ParlaMint-RS_2009-07-20-0.u24514	Ladies and gentlemen Members of the People, dear friends, at the very beginning I would like to mention the big action "Let's Clean Serbia", which I believe you are familiar with, which was initiated by this ministry. In order for this action to be well received and to prepare the basis for Serbia to become and remain a clean country, only a few months ago I had the honour of explaining a set of 16 environmental laws in this home. Today we are doing another great, I would say, and a historic step in the process that we can call – a major preservation of Serbia's spatial development. I want to say right away that Serbia's great storage cannot be reduced to law-making. First, we have an obligation to bring an enormous number of sublegal acts, which will detailed the procedures and processes that Serbia needs to make a regulated country. These sublegal acts are for the first package of law 188, and for the package of laws that you will adopt these days over 60. But changing Serbia's face requires much more effort than changing the rules. First and foremost, it requires respect and law enforcement, as well as a change in our relationship to the area in which we live today. These two things, respect for regulations and a more responsible relationship to the space that surrounds us, are things that most of the regulated countries mean. In Serbia, they are among the biggest problems of our transition. Fortunately, we have a plan regarding both of these challenges. The elements of that plan regarding respect for the law are next. Our commitment is to make the rules as simple as possible and reduce the burden and costs of respecting the rules for both citizens and the economy, as much as possible, without jeopardizing the space where we all live. The second point of the plan is that what is the law, or regulation, is really the law, and that we will insist on its implementation. No one should question this country's determination to apply its laws. As far as our relationship to living space is concerned by our citizens, the third point of our strategy is to work here from the basics, and our foundation is children. We want the system of values of our children to reflect the fact that the issue of the arrangement of the space in which their present and their future live is at stake. So education and education on space, if it doesn't already start in the family, must start from the earliest school age. Ladies and gentlemen of the People's Parliament, I would like to present to you with a little more detail that this package of laws will contribute to Serbia becoming a state-of-the-art. The proposal for a law on regulation of space and construction provides for broad and far-reaching reforms in this area, which rest on four pillars. Insisting on the coverage of Serbia's entire territory with planned documents, the regulation of property-legal relations on building land, a simpler procedure for issuing building permits and a more efficient process for legalising buildings. The lack of planning documents is a source of the arbitration of the administration in deciding who can build in Serbia and who can't. This arbitration is the basis of all corruption in the field of construction. Regarding this problem, the bill provides for a double strategy. On the one hand, the number of mandatory planning documents is reduced, the number of mandatory spatial plans has been reduced from five to four, and the number of mandatory urban plans has been reduced from four to two, or three in exceptional situations. As the goal that this is to achieve is to facilitate the obligations of state authorities, including local self-government, to meet the obligations related to the planning documents. Another way to ensure better coverage of Serbia's territory is to introduce more effective sanctions for those units of local self-government that do not meet their legal obligations. Local leadership, which is unable to secure the adoption of two mandatory planning documents, is unable to carry out its basic duty to local self-government. The deadlines we predicted for this job were carefully measured. They're real and doable. Therefore, there will be no justification for those local leaderships who intend to wake up in a year or two and say that there is not enough time or resources to make plans. On the contrary, work on the adoption of plans must take place immediately after the law enters into force, especially when it comes to the fact that the issue of building permits will only be possible on the basis of the planning documents. Local self-government which does not adopt two mandatory planning documents, which are the spatial plan of the unit of local self-government and the general regulation plan within 18 months and 24 months respectively, will not be able to issue either local or construction permits. The second pillar of the reform concerns the regulation of property-legal relations on building land. The bill envisions a deep and far-reaching reform in the area. The most important newspaper is the creation of conditions for the implementation of the new Constitution, according to which the building land can be in all forms of property. This bill allows rightholders to use and lease long-term rights to convert that right into property rights. In doing so, earlier owners of nationalised assets are predicted to have the right to property on construction land of the fee now have the right to use without compensation. On the other hand, those who are entitled to use construction land acquired through privatisation processes will have to pay compensation for this conversion. This proposal provides for the amount of compensation to be determined on the basis of the only fair criterion, based on the difference between the market value of the building land and the price paid for its acquisition. I'm sure it's not possible to suggest a model of conversion that would be fairer and more enforceable than this given in the bill of law that is before you today. The third pillar of the reforms adopted by this law is to simplify the process of issuing building permits. The World Bank reference list, comparing the quality of the world's legal framework for innovation, places Serbia 94th among the 181 world economy. That place isn't good and it has to change. But the fact that Serbia is inaccessible 171 when it comes to obtaining building permits is the alarm that called us for a deep reform of this procedure and a list of rational priorities. Simpler procedures for obtaining building permits have two important purposes. The first is to improve the competitiveness of the economic environment in Serbia, and the second is to reduce the cost and time of waiting for a building permit and thus to stop the practice of illegal construction from becoming a rule and become a socially unacceptable anomaly. Acceleration of the construction process will be provided in the following way: increasing the number of objects for which no construction permit is issued, making a difference in the scope of the technical documentation by type of object, removing one phase in obtaining the works necessary for construction, by issuing the building permit after obtaining the location permit, only the main project is attached, leading to a double time of obtaining the building permit and less price for the construction of the project, the portability of the building permit and the greater responsibility of all participants in the process, where the designer and the performer of the technical control are liable to criminal responsibility, if they verify the main project which has been carried out contrary to the construction rules of the location permit. The last, fourth pillar of the new law is the legalization procedure. Legalization of objects is an important objective of this law and those who have objects that are yet to be legalized can expect much from it. If in Serbia the number of illegally built buildings is estimated at about a million, it will not be that all these citizens, with their families and friends who helped them build, are criminals. It would rather be that the system was such that the citizens were forced into breaking the law if they wanted to provide a roof over the head for their families. This law will regulate the legalisation of objects in a much more efficient way than is now the case. The ministry's intention is to make legalisation mass and fair, with mass meaning that all the facilities for which there is a possibility will be legalized with a minimum of documentation. Fair legalisation means that there will be differences in the amount of compensation between buildings built for their own needs and buildings built for the market. It is imperative to end all legalisation procedures in two years from the date of entry into force of this law. Ladies and gentlemen, MPs, the current state of construction is costing us all a lot. Because of the lack of planning documents, unresolved ownership relations on building land and the long-term issue of construction permits, with a high degree of corruption, our citizens and the state of Serbia pay a large price. Young people who plan to buy an apartment pay the price of squares, which is higher than in most European countries because of various monopolys and included costs of corruption. Citizens who want to build their roof over their heads face a procedure they can't even guess how long it'll take. Investors who consider investing in Serbia the first thing they hear is that they cannot acquire property on land, but only the right to rent. Every day in an extension of this system, costs and losses are accumulated, and economic recovery slows down and delays. Each of us should contribute to making Serbia come out of the economic crisis more competitive, as a destination for investment than it has entered the crisis with a legal framework that will allow citizens to build faster and more efficiently and cheaply. This law is one of the most important steps to achieve this. The next act, which I have the honour of explaining to you, is the bill on social housing. The definition of the Government of the Republic of Serbia is to create conditions for dynamic economic growth and European integration, but not to forget those who, without the support of the state, would be condemned to chronic poverty and life below the human dignity line. With that in mind, after almost 20 years of complete inactivity, the state is returning to the social housing sector by this law. Many reasons for the adoption of social housing laws, and most generally it can be said that the area of social housing is almost entirely regulated, and that the changes that took place in fiscal legislation have significantly deteriorated the conditions for the development of this sector, abolishing sources of funding for social housing. Also, according to laws governing the affairs of local self-government, the launch of housing programmes has mostly been transferred to their jurisdiction, but without predicting real resources to fulfil these tasks. This results in continued non-resolvement of social housing needs and accumulation of problems. Along with the general decline in living standards, housing needs have increased significantly in the very part of the population that needs support in resolving the housing issue. The purpose of the law is to provide conditions for sustainable development of social housing, i.e. to find appropriate ways to address housing issues that cannot provide housing under market conditions. This law first and foremost establishes the basis, institutional preconditions and strategic framework for the development of the social housing system in the Republic of Serbia in order to address housing issues more effectively and the category of population. The national social housing strategy should define the main objectives of the government's socio-resident policy, as well as the appropriate measures and priorities for achieving these objectives. It will represent a framework for the adoption of concrete programmes on the basis of which the financial plan will be adopted and the various sources of funding animated. This kind of funding program provides good visibility and flexibility, as well as the ability to control, which is particularly important for the functioning of this system. Local housing strategies will identify local social housing needs and the interest of municipalities to solve housing problems. The housing programmes stemming from local strategies will form the basis for the issue of funding for these needs in the local budget, but also for obtaining additional funding from the republican level for their realisation, which will depend on how much they are included in national priorities. It is important to say that users of social housing rights will be defined by this law, and according to the following criteria of the order of the championship – housing status, remuneration height, health status, disability, physical damage and the number of household members. The next act we propose to the National Assembly as part of a package of regulations, which aim at a major preservation of Serbia's spatial development, is a proposal for a law on state diameter and catastro. Meeting European integration, Serbia is in a position to adopt a modern European law on state-owned diameter and cataster, which brings up-to-date solutions and which is fully in line with all European standards in this area. The application of the Act of State Crossing and the cadastre and the registration of the right to immovable property has been applied for 15 years. Meanwhile, there have been significant technological and other changes on the one hand, while on the other hand the growing demands of the economy and citizens for greater efficiency of the cadastrian system are growing. The new Law on State Prime Minister and Catastro contains standards that will meet the new modern challenges that all observed shortcomings in the current law have been addressed. The law will increase the security, accuracy and transparency of all records kept by the Republic Geodetic Institute, and in particular the registration of immovable property and real rights over them in the building block. This law has given state diameter its full meaning and its European dimension through a new classification. The new cadastrian diameters will be realised in order to establish a cadastrian immovableity, especially in areas with old state diameters carried out at the end of the 19th century, and thus Serbia will receive an up-to-date and up-to-date cadastrian record. This law modernly regulates the area of topographic diameter and cartographic performance in Serbia. Data solutions are adapted to technical and technological achievements in this area and are in line with European standards. The harmonisation of the law with the legal system of the Republic of Serbia has defined the jurisdiction of the Republic Geodesy Institute as a civil institution over the diameter of the state border and the keeping of the register of state borders. The building site has been established so far, i.e. 82% of Serbia's territory, i.e. in 89% of the catastrian municipalities in the Republic of Serbia. In order for this job to be successfully implemented in general by the end of 2010, the new law allows for faster creation of a catastra of immovableity, through the simplification of cadastrian procedures, the possibility of creating a catastra of immovableity at the individual request of the owner, as well as the application and introduction of new information technologies. The law also introduces a new state reference system that provides a very precise positioning of parcels, objects, water, topographical details in space, with an accuracy of several centimeters, thanks to the application of global navigation satellite systems. This system will be used to the greatest extent by private geodetic organisations, which will take time for geodetic measurements on the ground, for example, to record the newly built building, to be multiplely shorter, and only measurement will be much more accurate. Ladies and gentlemen of the People's Parliament, I would like to explain to you - a bill on the environmental fund, which I expect to become one of the most active factors in achieving the goal I announced at the beginning of this address, which is – a major preservation of Serbia's spatial development. According to one review of the state of the UN environment, the government should revise short- and medium-term budgetary plans on the distribution of environmental protection funds. The aim is to ensure that an adequate part of public revenues is directed to the Ministry of Environmental Protection, as well as to the Environmental Protection Fund, as well as to strengthen the Environment Fund to human and financial resources. The Environmental Protection Fund was established by the Environmental Protection Act to provide financial resources to promote protection and promotion of the environment in the Republic. Its establishment is in line with all the recommendations we have received from adequate international institutions. Experience from transition countries shows that the establishment of the Environment Fund has enabled the collection of significant funds and acted as a catalyst for important investments, through the approval of favourable loans and grants to invest in environmental protection. In these countries, the operations of the Fund are governed by a special law and we follow that practice. This proposal regulates the functioning of the Fund on a new basis, given that a set of laws in the field of environmental protection has established new incomes, and thus new competences of the Fund. In this connection, the need for a new concept of operation, i.e. organisation of waste and packaging business, fisheries, as well as other areas, has arisen. The proposal achieves the general objectives set out in the law, i.e. the correct application of established principles – the integration of the policy of protection and improvement of life and economic policy and the application of economic instruments and other measures promoting the quality of the environment; the application of the principles is ensured – the pay polluter, in accordance with EU standards and the recommendations of relevant international organisations. This focuses on sources of pollution and opens up the possibility of applying economic instruments and other measures that improve the quality of the environment. This should lead to an increase in the level of investment in the environment in order to cover the costs of work, maintenance, modernisation and expansion of existing infrastructure in the field of environmental protection and pollution reduction technologies. This law introduces new mechanisms for the use of funds from the Fund, through incentive measures, which are planned by means of a set of environmental laws. It also provides for the possibility of initiating and self-managing projects by the Fund, in order to respond more effectively to the present problems in the environment. New funding instruments are introduced that meet the needs of the modern economy, as well as the new competences of the Fund, such as the provision of services from the work cycle. As one measure of rationalising business in the current conditions, a reduction of the control apparatus is proposed. The number of board members is reduced from seven to five, and the supervisory board is abolished because the ministry is overseeing the work. Ladies and gentlemen, with thanks for the attention you have given to this set of laws of long-term importance for our country's development and the special gratitude for the amendments you have submitted, many of which we consider not only acceptable but also very useful, we would like to invite you to vote in favour of the laws that have been proposed to you.

In this law, it is not limited to how many years it must be passed, so the SRS has proposed an amendment to limit it to a maximum of 25 years, so that we do not get into a situation to bring the spatial plan of the Republic of Serbia for a period of 50 or 70 years, which is an extremely high deadline. As for the spatial plan of units of local self-government, it states that it contains particularly parts of the territory for which urban planning and urban project are envisaged. I don't think it should stand here - an urban project, because at the level and scale of the local self-government unit's spatial plan, from one to 25 to one to 50,000, it's not possible to predict even spatially determine the parts of the territory that need to be done by an urban project that's ranging from one to 500,000. Also, in this law we have more mistakes, we have in Articles 20 and 22 that the already defined content repeats itself in Article 29. Also, what I find extremely important is that, for the spatial plan of the unit of local self-government and the general urban plan, as it stands here, there is a previous consensus before reference to public insight, as well as agreement on those plans before the publication of the minister responsible for matters of spatial planning and urbanism. I find it absolutely unnecessary to give consent to a plan document adopted by the authority in accordance with its competences. Consent opens up the possibility of abuse of position, delaying procedures and opening the possibility of corruption. An individual, even if he was a minister, should not decide on documents adopted by the competent authorities, especially as there are other documents for the control of legality, the drafting of plans, such as the Commission for Plans, the urban inspector, the higher order planning documents, etc. In particular, we know in Serbia what is happening and that there is a possibility that the minister from one party will not want to give consent to one of the plans of the municipality because there is a completely different political option there. Why can graduate space planners who graduated from college 15 years ago or 20 years ago not, as so far, be responsible urbanists, why are they denied this right? It also states here in the law that construction land, among other things, can be the one on which buildings are built contrary to the law and land on which only temporary objects are built. I think this definition is completely wrong, that unbuilt building land can only be the one on which there are absolutely no objects. Furthermore, as regards the technical examination of the facility carried out after the construction of the facility, it does not appear within the law what time limit it is to carry out the technical examination. We have suggested that it be 30 days, because we believe it is extremely important that there is no delay in carrying out that technical examination when the facility is finished. We believe that this law has many shortcomings and therefore the SRS has proposed a large number of amendments. I hope that the ruling majority will adopt a large number of amendments and will thus improve this law on planning and arrangement of space and construction.

ParlaMint-RS_2009-07-20-0.u24522	Ladies and gentlemen of the People's Parliament, the Honored Presidency, the Honorable Ministers, the Ministry's representatives, I have issued my opinion on the Committee on Labour, Combat and Social Affairs, and on the social housing law. In that sense, I would like to ask you some questions on behalf of the citizens of Serbia. The social housing law, ladies and gentlemen, is one of the most important laws for all the social groups in our country. The draft social housing law was made on the basis of various documents in the first version at the end of 2004, so that only in 2007 it would be adopted by the government and sent to parliament for consideration. We mean that social housing, housing of the relevant standards provided with the support of the state, and in line with the social housing strategy, the programme for the realization of the same and is intended for households and natural persons who, for social, economic and other reasons, cannot provide themselves with an apartment on market terms. The PUPS Group also considers it to be a maximum participation, in collaboration with the Group 484, a group of the Urban Development Programmes, with the Institute of Sustainable Community, and with a large number of organisations to improve social housing of endangered groups and associations of solidar housing, established common principles and concepts which would have to represent this important base system law, and this: firstly, that public intervention covers a much wider range of measures needed for social housing than proposed; secondly, that the entire system is designed to divide competences in financing social housing from the central level and implementation of housing programmes at local level; thirdly, that the system of financing of social housing is based on mandatory reimbursement of costs; fourthly, that all subsidies in the social housing system must be general and public and fifthly, that the social housing system must possess a non-performing capacity in real estate schemes. The definition of the social housing of the European Association of National Associations of Housing Organisations is binding in this area because it argues that the housing in which the approach is controlled by the existence of rules on the allocation of dwellings and aimed at households that have difficulty providing housing on the market is the only proper policy. What is defined by Article 19 is that funds must be used on purpose. If it is not used on purpose, if it is determined, then those funds must be returned to the fund's budget. The proposed fund law improves the system of environmental management and management, because we will bring new planning documents and because we will have safe and long-term sources of funding for projects in the field of environmental protection.

As for spatial plans, I will talk about spatial plans in Serbia, which are of great importance to our country's development. I will first mention and discuss the spatial plan of the Republic of Serbia, which as a strategic development document sets out the long-term foundations of the organisation, the use and arrangement of the territory of the Republic of Serbia, the direction of urbanisation and the basic criteria of settlement arrangements, the principles and criteria for the use of natural resources and the protection of the environment, the conditions for the protection and use of areas of particular importance, the corridors of basic infrastructure systems. As far as the spatial plan of the Republic of Serbia is concerned, it is subject to the distribution of agricultural land use, deforestation, habitats and game breeding centres, water springs and water infrastructures, metals and nemetas, energy sources, energy infrastructure, population space, centre networks, industrial space, intensive development belts, possible areas of impact of macro-regional centres, road network, rail network, telecommunications, tourism, environmental protection, nature protection and ecosystems, protection of fixed cultural goods. The word plan itself means a picture of the desired or imagined projected state. It's a statistical character. Planning is a process that lasts and is dynamic. Only spatial planning is a decision-making process for an appropriate action in the future through a series of elections, is also a process by which a particular course of action is chosen to achieve the objectives set. As far as spatial plans are concerned, the first spatial plans created were regional. The first spatial plan is in Rohr, then Tennessee Valley in the U.S., which began in the early '40s. Thereafter, spatial plans arise for the expansion of cities or urban regions of one or more cities, large London, a system of cities on the Atlantic coast, a later attempt to decentralisation the functions of Paris and so on. In other words, spatial planning in modern understanding has evolved from regional spatial planning. As far as our country is concerned, the first spatial plan that arose is the spatial plan of the Danube coast from Belgrade to the Bulgarian border, built between 1961 and 1963 in connection with the construction of HE Đerdap. The idea of this plan was to analyse and record the relations and situations that existed before HE and then find optimal solutions to the building of space, given the positive and negative effects of building HE. As for the hydropower plant itself, which was a total length of 968 meters, conclusions were made on the effects of the construction of the hydropower plant itself. These are the natural conditions of intervention in the immersion of existing valleys and the presence of a more significant water surface for the plant world, the impact on the rise of groundwater levels in the wider area and the measures for their neutralisation - a special impact on the entire sea basin, which must be regulated according to new conditions. As for traffic, the full satisfaction of the future river traffic in the Danube region, through the dam, has enabled the integration of Yugoslavia with Romania both rail and road traffic; in particular, the potential for traffic development and traffic networks in eastern Serbia according to general conditions of development. As for cities and populations, some existing settlements have been submerged, and new settlements have been built as their replacement and the reception of a new population. The general increase in wealth, driven by new facilities and the employment of new people and richer works, imposes more quantitative and even more quality development of settlements of all categories. Also, as regards the importance of spatial plans, in order for the spatial plan to be good, it is necessary to form the information base of the spatial plan, that is, first of all, to collect available study-analytical, plan-development and other documentation, then to form documentation on the physical-geographical and biogeographical characteristics of the area, to form documentation on the population and the workforce, data on the structure of the economy and socio-economic development. Then, as far as building land is concerned, the data of the total land area is intended for housing, then the area of land intended for industry, which is intended for trade, catering and tourism, for trade, for traffic, for construction and similar activities, then for education, health, social housing, land intended for public and administrative purposes, for communal needs, for recreation and entertainment, parks and park woods, playground specially designed for land. As for the population, migration and employment, the number and rate of birth and mortality, the population according to age and gender, the mechanical growth of the population and migration, the employment of the population are extremely important. As far as housing is concerned, an important specification of annual housing, housing costs per meter of a square apartment, land construction, is an extremely important conversion of agricultural and forest land into urban construction, population density in certain areas – city center, periphery, municipalities etc., built area of fixed funds, technical infrastructure, where water system and water consumption are involved, sewerage system, energy system, traffic system. Social and living standards of the population within which the number of preschool children is, the number of children includes compulsory schooling, the number of schools, the number of students, the number of teachers, the number of doctors per thousand residents, the number of beds in hospitals, the number of cinemas, theatres, the literacy of the population, the number of libraries. These are all data that are of great importance to the work of spatial plans and the quality of the human environment, i.e. land pollution, noise, air pollution and water quality. That's the answer to the other parliamentary group that's asking why we're now retroactively charging something. We don't charge retroactively, they bought a company, brand, employees, machines, buildings, but they never bought the right property over that land, they bought only the right to use, as long as there's some established activity that this company deals with. You want to change your performance, you want to convert and turn that land into a quality building plot on which you're going to build commercial content, you're going to have to pay. It's the most honest model possible and we represent it here today. ParlaMint-RS_2009-07-20-0.u24544	Dear Madam Chairman, Mr Minister, ladies and gentlemen of the People's Parliament, there are four very important legislative proposals in front of us today, and as a member of the Committee on Environmental Protection, I will first say something about the proposal for a law on the environmental fund. I'll remind you that we recently passed a whole set of laws in this area dealing not only with protection but also with environmental damages. In order for all these laws not only to remain a dead letter on paper, we were then announcing that the envisaged environmental fund law was passed. The fund created in the adoption of this law aims, on the one hand, rationally and in the best possible way to spend funds collected from economic operators bearing polluter status. On the other hand, the fund is also a provision of legal certainty to economic operators who settle their obligations to the institutions of the Republic of Serbia, which in the future will have a very significant impact on the decision-making of an investor in the event of investment of their capital in our country. So, by adopting an already valid set of laws, we have allowed all preventive measures, as well as criminal provisions, to rest on the principle of wage polluter. In order to raise funds directly from contaminants, it is necessary to establish a fund in which these funds will be collected and then, according to plans, projects and needs, to make the same funds available to the environments that are necessary. The existence of such a fund does not just mean providing quality management of dedicated funds under the law, but rather enables its existence to compete with numerous international financial institutions, as well as with various major global foundations, which are aimed at protecting the environment. The opportunities ahead of the foundation of this fund are indeed numerous. This best confirms world practice, on the basis of which we can conclude that the adoption of laws on environmental funds and their implementation has contributed to the achievement of the objectives of life and work protection, the preservation of natural balance, the rational use of natural resources, the use of renewable resources, and, most importantly, to ensure the development of human health. I believe that investment in the environment is an investment not only in our future, but also in the future of our children and all generations to come. The value of this law certainly also gives transparency, i.e. the public that it provides for in the allocation of funds, as well as the public in determining the criteria on which these funds will be allocated, as well as their use. As an MP from Loznica, I will point out the importance of adopting this law for local environments most affected by various types of pollution. I hope that in the near future, there will be an opportunity to resuscitate and relocate the city's dump, located on the edge of Loznica, and now it has spread so much that it takes up part of the city itself. In addition to the efforts so far made by the local self-government, as well as great understanding by the resolution ministries, this problem is still waiting for a solution. The causal inability to solve such or similar problems lies precisely in the incompetence of already existing funds. Knowing the problems of environmental threats in other cities and municipalities, I don't think Loznica differs with its problems in this important area, but I just want to point out how important it is for the adoption of such a law for each local self-government. Only the existence of this fund allows access to international financial flows, which will bring the greatest benefit to local self-government, and thus to all citizens of the Republic of Serbia. Another legal proposal I'd like to talk about today is a bill on social housing. Unfortunately, the period of transition, the war facts in the old Yugoslavia, whose consequences have not been removed so far in Serbia and the increasingly present consequences of the global economic crisis, have conditioned a very unfavourable social map of our country's population. For these reasons, we in Serbia today have a socially endangered population group, which, for social, economic and other reasons, is difficult for itself and for its consequences, provides existence, with naturally unresolved housing issues. Because of all this, I find it extremely important that today's proposal for a social housing law is in front of us. I will only remind myself that social housing implies housing of a certain standard, which is provided with the support of the state and is intended for households which, for social, economic and other reasons, cannot provide themselves with an apartment on market conditions. The adoption of a national strategy provided for by this law will contribute greatly to the resolution of a large number of problems of this kind. Also, the establishment of a republican housing agency, as well as local organisations of the same purpose, will certainly contribute to the quality of rerouting of funds both from the state and local level. The value of such a long-term and institutionalised approach to this important problem, aimed at achieving a sustainable social balance, certainly lies in greater access to foreign donor organisations, as well as numerous specialised international agencies dealing with both professional and financial assistance in solving all these problems. The criteria provided for in this law, which in future will determine the status of persons who meet the conditions for some of the types of social housing, are – current housing status, income height, disability, number of household members, etc. It is also important that all organisations that will implement the social housing strategy, as this law provides, must be of a non-profit character. I would take this opportunity to pass on the positive experience of the passwordd self-government of this kind to this home. Today when we talk about this law, we have confirmation that this is the way of housing solving the most socially endangered categories possible. In Loznica, we already have two residential buildings of this kind, which have solved housing problems slightly less than 25 of the most socially endangered families. It is about projects that have been launched based on successful co-operation of local self-government and foreign donor houses, with the help of the Ministry of Labour and Social Policy. The first building, or residential building, is funded thanks to the Japanese government's help. The purpose of these apartments is to resolve the housing issue of wartime military invalids. Local self-government secured the location and built a complete accompanying infrastructure. The second facility was achieved thanks to the assistance of the Swiss SDC, and the purpose of this donation was to focus on the housing of exiled and refugees. The experience gained by the management of the Loznica through the implementation of these projects is enormous, and this also points to the highest rating of both donors and the competent ministry. We have already been convinced in part of the positive effects that the legislator has stated in his explanation. In doing so, I am primarily referring to the provision of socially endangered groups of people, but it must not be ignored even the positive results arising from the partnership of the public and private sector engaged in the construction of buildings. I sincerely believe that the adoption of this law will contribute not only to the formal filling of the legal vacuum created by the termination of the Law on Tax on the Fund, which financed solidarity construction by 2004, but that the implementation of this law will successfully solve the accumulated problems of the socially endangered category of the population in all municipalities and cities of Serbia. I believe that such examples as Loznica, and today we have heard the Kings, there are many more, and that after the adoption of this law, there will be even more, where, with good co-operation from the local self-government ministry and with the help of donors, the problems of the poorest layers of the population will be solved. I believe that in these difficult times, we must show collective responsibility and help those who have the most difficulty. At the very end, I would like to say that in the voting day the G17 Parliament Club will support all four legislative proposals.

ParlaMint-RS_2009-07-20-0.u24548	The President-in-Office, respected representatives of the ministry, fellow MPs, in a set of laws that we are considering today dominates the law on the regulation of space and construction, and this is certainly one of the most important laws that this assembly should adopt. We are witnessing the urban chaos that governs the good part of our country, the spatial irregularities, inadequate maintenance of construction heritage and cultural goods, as well as protected natural continents. The reason for this is the lack of plans, as well as the overcoming of existing plans that continue to apply and the non-compliance with laws and regulations. All of this has caused the need to pass a new, modern law, which will try to solve these problems and, in that sense, arrange our space. What is very important about the bill is the fact that it really simplifies and makes plans and implements them, i.e. the process of obtaining a building permit. We are all well aware of the fact that Serbia is among the countries with the slowest administration, especially when it comes to the process of obtaining a building permit. It is now important to shorten that time under the new bill, by simplifying the process of issuing a building permit and by far less documents needed to obtain a building permit, as well as a shortened period of time for which the responsible persons in the authorities of the administration have to issue those documents. The number of plans available to territorial units, both spatial and urban, has been reduced. What is very important is what plans must be made for units of local self-government and within what time period, and through the Law on Local Self-Government, sanctions will be laid down for municipalities that do not adopt the necessary spatial and urban plans within a certain period. In other words, if a municipality does not adopt a spatial plan of the municipality and a plan of general regulation, it will be punished, sanctioned by the introduction of provisional measures. So we finally give importance to the planning acts at that level as well as to the budget. It is also very important to establish a hierarchy of documents of spatial and urban planning. They must be consistent with each other, so that the documents of the narrow area must be in line with the plans of the wider area, and all together must be absolutely in line with the Space Plan of the Republic of Serbia. The design of the planning documents is managed by responsible planners and responsible urbanists, who receive appropriate licences from the Engineering Chamber. Under the 2003 law, the responsible urbanist's license could be obtained by persons of the proper profession, including graduate spatial planners. However, this bill deprives them of this right and puts them in an unequal position compared to other technical professions. I think that, in this sense, a correction should be made in law and spatial planners to restore the same opportunities that they had so far, i.e. the possibility of obtaining and licensing a responsible urbanist. What is very important is the new law also introduces the existence of a central register of plans, documents to be conducted by the competent ministry. All plans to be adopted under this law will be recorded in this register and thus made available to all. So anyone who wants to get information -- what is the purpose of the space and what urban parameters apply anywhere in Serbia -- will be able to see it on the Internet or how it will be organised by the Ministry. In this way, among other things, any abuse of information about a location is prevented, and we have witnessed largely for all these years. Otherwise, these are new concepts that appear in this law – location information and location permit. Information on the location is closest to the previous urban purpose report, and the location permit is a document on the basis of which technical documentation is made, i.e. a document containing all technical data and all urban parameters that are sufficient for the designer to produce the main project. Detailed construction land, the shape of its property, the way it was used. What I would specifically single out is a problem related to the construction of building land. The law stipulates that compensation is paid for the construction land according to decisions and programmes adopted by local self-government units. The law also stipulates that these funds, therefore, obtained from the compensation for the construction land, are used exclusively on purpose. However, I am responsibly saying that this is not the case in practice. Very often these funds are used for some completely different things, which is the worst, very often go for salaries of people who are employed in local self-government. I think it would be necessary, in that sense, to find a way to control it, because if some general control was carried out at the level of all Serbia, in all municipalities, I'm afraid these claims would be confirmed. Perhaps the solution to this problem would be to create an obligation in a certain law to raise funds collected from the compensation for the regulation of construction land to be income exclusively to public enterprises that care for the arrangement of construction land, and whose founders are certain territorial units. In fact, there is the biggest problem with those public enterprises whose founder is the local self-government unit. It is only in this way that we would allow these funds to be really used for the purpose, that is to be used for the construction land, for the acquisition of building land and for the construction and maintenance of infrastructure, which is necessary for complete building land. The very important newspaper that this law introduces is the possibility of converting the right to use into property rights with or without compensation. This is how a certain kind of restitution is done to the extent that this can be done through the law governing space and construction. What has greatly affected the poor situation in the spatial and urban arrangements throughout Serbia is certainly a poor work of inspection services, I mean, first of all, construction inspection. The existence of a large number of illegal objects and the accidental use of space tells about the problem of abuse and malfeasance. This law gives greater authority to the construction inspection and its adequate application will significantly affect changes in the existing situation. So, the work of construction, of course, and urban inspection and full respect for the legislation will have the most impact on the proper editing and use of space. One of the most common topics in this area is certainly the legalisation of illegally built objects. Therefore, a continuation of this process, which has been stopped for a time, is envisaged, by detailing which objects can enter the legalisation process, with which documentation and within what time period. In this respect, it should certainly be borne in mind that law-abiding builders have threatened the planned spatial and urban development of the area in which they were building and that this practice should be permanently contained. One way is certainly the inability to attach illegal objects to an infrastructure network, as well as to punish an official who commits an illegal facility to any infrastructure network. Penalties detail the methods of punishment for certain offences. However, the impression is that some of the offences are not adequately ranked. For example, the level of penalties for offences when a responsible official in the competent authority of the administration, past the law, issues a locational, construction or use permit cannot certainly be leveled with the level of penalties for infringements when the same official does not issue that document within the prescribed time limit. So the degree of social danger and consequences that can arise when documents are issued beyond laws and plans are certainly much higher, and this must also be highlighted at the level of punishment. In that sense, I have submitted amendments and I hope to come to an understanding by the Ministry. However, regardless of some objections, this is without a doubt a reformist law, a law that opens up opportunities to address many of the problems burdening Serbia's spatial and urban development and the ZES parliamentary group will declare their adoption in the day of voting.

An example of controversial privatisation, based on non-compliance with the law, abuse of urban planning and identification or lack of development priorities, is Luka Belgrade. This port is unique for many things: first - it is the last to reach sea-river ships; second - it has the appropriate manipulative capacity; third - it has the possibility of multiple capacity increases; fourth - it is located at the intersection of international corridors 10 and 7 with international waterways Sava and Tis; fifth - located close to the base station in Makisha and two major technical performance airports "Nikola Tesla" and "Batajnica". All of this makes the port of Belgrade more competitive than other ports all the way down to Constanta and any transfer of the port downstream would lead to a significant reduction in competitiveness, while moving to the other coast of the Danube and detaching from existing infrastructure would completely destroy the competitiveness of the port. Its current position makes "Luku Belgrade" a basic economic entity and an enker of Belgrade's future development. The main activity is the manipulation of containers, with a capacity that has now fallen from 13 to 15 thousand after privatisation to about 10,000 containers a year. When I mean manipulation, you know what kind of manipulation I mean, not the one someone would think of. The size of the port, the length of the berths and the gauze which enables the hand-holding of the sea-river ship and barges, along with the necessary technical and technological rehabilitation, can increase the port's capacity by at least 30 times, to a minimum of 300,000 containers per year. The idea of our politicians to become a regional service centre, the most mentioned banking, is only possible if a significant commodity flow is going through a regional centre, or through the city. Apart from the port, no other activity in Belgrade's territory can provide an adequate commodity flow that would stimulate any accompanying service activity. Despite all the comparative advantages and even the current size of business, the owner of the "Luke Belgrade" decides to build a business and housing complex in that place. Legally, the owner's right implies that he does what he wants with his firm, but the government's obligation is to protect the interests of its citizens, first from selling a strategic economic operator to an unconscious buyer, then finally eliminating the same strategic entity, and at the expense of the state, the citizens of Belgrade and Serbia, who will pay for it. For example, it should be stated that there are now between two and three million square meters of commercial space in central Belgrade, some 250,000 square metres of ground floor space. Will the value of that commercial space and the revenue from its exploitation fall when a new 800 thousand square meters in the port appears on the market? Will the owners of this unsuspecting area sue the state and demand compensation based on lost profits, but also capital loss for the dilettant planning of the city's development that led to this? Who will be responsible for reducing budget revenues based on property tax and capital gains tax, if that property loses part of the current value and capital gains decreases? In spite of all, the city administration is making a decision to initiate the procedure for the change of the general urban plan, adopted in 2003, with a duration of up to 2021, which would, among other things, require the removal of the port and set a new location for the port, and based on a competitive solution, which was made for the needs of the "Luke Belgrade" company. Not taking into account and not respecting economic and infrastructure parameters, i.e. state interests and citizens' interests, the city's administration has complied with capital requirements, which by definition pursues only profit, not taking into account the permanent damage that will be done to the public sector and to all citizens of our country. However, as a result of the planning documentation, the port in Belgrade is foreseen at a location where the company "Luka Belgrade" now exists, with the spacious plan of the Republic of Serbia, the Belgrade City's General Urban Plan adopted in 2003 and the Belgrade Regional Plan adopted in 2004. All these plans are higher order than a detailed urban or regulatory plan. Changes to the lower order plans are possible only if they are in agreement with the higher order plans. Possible changes can be initiated by the authorities of the city's or republic's administration, and in no case can potential investors through an internal competition for urban solution, which takes into account only the demands of the same investor, which is not only the "Luke Belgrade" but also almost all major ventures in Belgrade in the last eight years. As an example, I quote "The Palace of Usce", Autocommand, Shipyard, Stari mlin, "Partizanka" etc. The abuse of competition by the investor leads to the investor's rule leading to a more favourable position, eliminating competition and harming citizens. The most serious issue regarding "Luka Belgrade" is about changing the right to use construction land. In Serbia, the state is the only owner of construction land, regardless of whether it is acquired by its means or nationalisation. The same state has the right only to enter into the use of building land, exclusively related to urbanism established for the purpose of a building built on that land and no user has the right to property over assigned building land. This means that any change in the purpose of using construction land by users practically means that land in a legally prescribed procedure must be granted to the most favourable bidder, which is bound to pay compensation for the convenience of the location, to bring the location to a new purpose, to the costs of the organisation of the terrain, primary and secondary infrastructures. Ownership of a company that at the time of privatisation had the right to use land, for example for industrial purposes, is not the basis for automatic, especially free, land use rights for the purpose of building business and housing facilities. In the event that the city's decision on the purpose of the land on which the company "Luka Belgrade" is operating is changed from industrial to residential, this means that the company "Luka Belgrade" should move from that location, because it would not have an automatic right to use. If the new purpose of building land is established, the State is obliged to apply the land through the prescribed procedure to the use of the most favourable bidder. The decision of the Constitutional Court of 2005 lays down that the provisions of Article 1 Decisions amending the Building Land Decision, which was published in "The Official Journal of the City of Belgrade" No. 18/2003, not in accordance with the Constitution and the law, and the explanation states: "It comes from those constitutional and legal provisions (President: Time. Here, I'm finishing up... . the state-owned means of issuing building land for lease, as a rule, by public tendering, i.e. by collecting offers by public advertising. “ Mr. Minister, this example clearly shows that whoever has the money, who owns a specific land, can do with it whatever he wants, buy it, tear it down, move it. I think it's embarrassing, and we can't let that happen.

ParlaMint-RS_2009-07-20-0.u24586	Dear Madam President of the National Assembly, ladies and gentlemen of the People's Parliament, representatives of the electoral will of Serbian citizens, it is my special honor to address you at the beginning of the debate on public health laws, transfusiological activity, organ transplants, tissue and cell transplants, as well as the law on the treatment of infertility by biomedical assisted fertilisation procedures. The Law on Public Health regulates the realisation of public interest by creating conditions for the preservation and improvement of the health of the population through comprehensive activities of society aimed at preserving the physical and mental health of the population, preserving the working and living environment, preventing the emergence and impact of risk factors for health, illness and injury, also the way and procedure, as well as the conditions for the organisation and implementation of public health. There are several regulations in the Republic of Serbia that directly or indirectly refer to health, and these are laws, and regulations, decisions, rules, orders and instructions. Important steps in Serbia to comply with EU rules in the field of public health were made by adopting a Sustainable Development Strategy and a Public Health Strategy adopted in March 2009. The public health strategy is a support in meeting social care for health, providing conditions in which people can be healthy and makes the basis for government decisions on actions to improve the health and quality of life of the population of the Republic of Serbia. The adoption of public health law is necessary in order to clearly define this area and bring into line with the existing legal assets "Aki Communitre", the role of the state in protecting public health interests, the way in which all the necessary participants are involved in the activities of harmonisation of public health interventions, the development of efficient public health infrastructure and the introduction of responsibilities for the financing of public health services by both the state and other sources. The public health law can therefore be defined as a set of relations, rights and duties by which the state provides conditions for the health of the population, identifies, prevents and reduces risks to the health of the population and limits the autonomy, privacy, ownership or other legally protected interests of the individual, with a view to safeguarding and promoting the health of the community. The law on transfusiological activity implies an important need to ensure that in the area of transfusion activity, i.e. planning, collection, blood processing and blood components, regardless of their purpose, as well as the preservation, management, distribution, supply and issue of blood and components intended for transfusions, the establishment of standards for the provision of adequate amounts of safe blood to the population of the Republic of Serbia, and that healthcare institutions carrying out this work enable better conditions to perform their work in accordance with modern achievements in this area. One of the objectives is certainly harmonisation with high standards, which is also recommended at this time by the Council of Europe and the European Commission in the field of planning, collection, blood processing and blood components, regardless of their intention, recommendations from the World Health Organisation, as well as by the European Parliament and the Council of Europe on the setting of standards of quality and safety in the collection, testing, processing, storage, management and distribution of blood and blood components, also technical guidelines on the preparation, use and security of blood components, pointing to the need to improve blood transfusion activity. The main reason is to provide adequate amounts of safe blood and blood components in the Republic of Serbia. The adoption of this law aims to establish new, more standards of health institutions that carry out this activity, i.e. certain tasks of this activity, as well as more standards of institutions that use blood and blood components in their work. By achieving this basic goal, it is necessary to ensure and achieve national self-satisfaction in the provision of safe blood and blood components. In addition to setting up an appropriate legal framework for transfusiological action at a higher level, in line with international body directives in this area, it is necessary to ensure coordination of measures and activities to strengthen voluntary giving, to improve the organisation and quality of work, to establish appropriate conditions regarding staff, equipment and premises, to establish an appropriate information system, to enable appropriate communication of all institutions, to prepare and implement continued training programmes for healthcare professionals, also to consider and optimise the financing of health institutions. A quality assurance programme is needed, which is also guaranteed by this regulation. In achieving the highest level of safe blood and blood components it is necessary to provide adequate conditions for the application of a closed blood-processing system and components in all health institutions that carry out the work of blood processing. The establishment of a quality system will be carried out in accordance with the standards of medical professions for collection, testing, processing, storage and distribution of blood and blood components. The adoption of this law aims to establish standards for the provision of adequate quality blood for patients as end-users. Citizens, i.e. patients as end-users, will be allowed quality and safety of blood and guarantee blood components collected, tested, produced, stored, distributed and issued in accordance with high medical standards, and for society in general is useful in establishing a higher quality in providing health care. For healthcare institutions, the adoption of this law shall provide the necessary conditions to enable, through a system of issue, i.e. the withdrawal of authorisations to carry out this activity, the highest standard in all procedures of collection, testing, production, storage, distribution and issue, in accordance with the conditions laid down by this law and the sublegal acts to be adopted for the implementation of this law and with medical standards. Health institutions will be able to achieve the highest level of safety, and the organisation of compulsory health insurance can expect the establishment of a system and obtaining and withdrawing authorisations from health institutions, i.e. parts of these institutions from the network plan, i.e. certain transfusiological activities. In accordance with all of this, the paper in the Bill's proposal refers to the provision of health care facilities with quality and safety blood and blood components, in accordance with medical standards, the arrangement of methods, procedures, conditions and organisation of transfusiological activities; the establishment of social care for the health of the Republic's population through the provision of conditions for the supply of the population in all parts of the country with sufficient quality and safety blood and components, the organisation of hospital blood banks in health institutions that carry out stationary work that stores both blood and blood components and pretransfusion tests for the needs of that institution. The establishment of a quality system, i.e. the withdrawal of permits, as well as the pursuit of transfusiological activity or certain tasks of this activity; the establishment of a quality system; the establishment of haemovgilance; the provision of security, monitoring and reporting of serious, unwanted events and unwanted reactions; the education of hospital transfusiological committees that monitor the application and management of blood and blood components in stationary health institutions; the establishment of appropriate blood storage, management and distribution systems of blood and components; the management of a single register of donors, with particular management of data on persons who are temporarily or permanently deprived of the ability to give blood; the management of appropriate documentation and records in the area of transfusiological activity, the establishment of an annual plan for the territory of the entire Republic of Serbia, the establishment of methodologies for determining the cost of blood treatment and blood components and the education of the biomedicinetry of the Ministry of Health. The organ transplant law regulates the taking of organs or parts of organs from a living or dead person for transplanting into another person's body for treatment, supervision of the implementation of this law and other issues of importance for the organisation and implementation of organ transplants or parts of the body. The reasons are that the system is regulated in the area of transplantation, i.e. the taking of organs or parts of organs from a living or dead person to transplant into another person's body for treatment, institution organisations, supervision, as well as other issues of importance. Then, to create conditions for organ transplants in accordance with the most modern standards of medical science and practice, since this area of medicine is the one that develops intensively and is constantly getting greater and greater treatment opportunities than previously incurable diseases. Then, to create conditions for the promotion of transplants at European and national level in order to inform and familiarise citizens with the importance of organ donation, to regulate the area of transplantation in line with EU rules in this area. The Transplant Law is in line with the strict recommendations of all international institutions, as well as the Convention on Human Rights in Biomedicine and the Framework Protocol for the Implementation of the Convention on Human Rights in Biomedicine. We note that the area of cell and tissue transplantation is governed by the proposal for this package of laws, in accordance with the latest recommendation of the EU Commission, the area of tissue and cell transplantation is governed by a special law, independent of the organ transplant law. Between 2000 and 2007 a total of 532 kidney transplants were made in Serbia, with 84 dead persons and 448 live donors. During that period, eight liver transplants were performed, seven from dead faces, one from a living donor. During 2008, a kidney transplant has been done at the Clinical Centre of Serbia in Belgrade, the Children's Clinic in Tirshovo, Belgrade, the Centre for Transplantation in Novi Sad, the Clinical Centre of Vojvodina, the clinic for nephrology of the Military-medical Academy, and this year at the clinic for Nephrology of the Clinical Centre Nis. Compared to modern experiences, in the European environment, organ giving in Serbia is two per million people, when it comes to taking organs from dead people. The adoption of this law will create conditions for promoting donation and raising citizens' awareness of the importance of transplants, as well as for organising health institutions at the job of transplants, thus increasing the number of transplants and creating conditions for improving the quality of health care provided, in line with modern standards of today's medical sciences and practices. The law on cell and tissue transplants regulates the acquisition, donation, testing, processing, preservation and storage, distribution of human cells and tissues intended for human use, the establishment of banks of cells and tissues, the supervision of the implementation of this law and the conduct of certain tasks of state administration in the field of cell and tissue transplantation, as well as other issues of importance for cell and tissue transplantation. The reasons are to systematically regulate the area of human tissue and cell transplants, to create conditions for human cell and tissue transplantation in accordance with modern, modern standards of science and practice, since this area of medicine is one of the most intensive developments, to also create conditions for the promotion of cell and tissue transplants at national and European level and to bring the area of cell and tissue transplantation into line with the positions of international institutions in this area. The area of cells and tissues is governed by a special law, in accordance with the recommendation of the EU Commission, that the area of cell and tissue transplantation should be governed by a special law, not subject to organ transplant law. All the countries of the European continent which have adopted laws that are uniquely regulated by cell and tissue transplants and organ transplants, are at this time harmonising their legislation with those Directives. The adoption of this law creates conditions for promoting donation and raising citizens' awareness of the importance of transplants, as well as for the higher level of organisation of health services to carry out these complicated procedures. During the period from 2000 to 2007 at the Clinical Centre of Vojvodina in Novi Sad, at the clinic for haematology VMA, at the Institute for Mother and Child Dr Vukan Čupić and at the Institute of Haematology of the Clinical Centre, a total of 283 bone marrow transplants were performed. At the Institute of Eye Diseases of the Clinical Centre in Belgrade in the Oocular Diseases clinic, VMA total of 286 corneal transplants. A bone transplant at the orthopaedic surgery and trauma clinic in the Vojvodina Clinical Center 231. A total of 11 ligament transplants. During 2008, 124 bone marrow transplants and also a bone transplant of 258, ligament at 131. So, the law on tissue and cell transplants, together with the organ transplant law, but in two separate regulations and under the organisation and supervision of a guaranteed law establishing a biomedicine administration in the Ministry of Health, should contribute to significant progress in both areas in the years ahead of us after the adoption of this law. The law on the treatment of infertility through biomedical assisted fertilisation procedures shall regulate the conditions, the manner and procedure of treatment of infertility in women and men, the procedures of biomedical assisted fertilisation for the conception of the child, the supervision of the implementation of this law and the conduct of certain matters of the state administration in this area, the treatment of fertility by biomedical assisted fertilisation, and other issues of importance for the implementation of procedures regulated by this regulation, the reasons for the systematic management of the area of treatment of infertility by the procedures of biomedical aided fertilisation, to ensure supervision of the implementation of this law and the conduct of State administration in the field of fertility treatment, as well as other issues regulated by this law. The adoption of this regulation will create conditions for the treatment of infertility by biomedical assisted fertilisation even more in line with modern standards of medical science and practice, as conditions will be created for a higher level of promotion of donation of reproductive cells at national level in order to inform and familiarise citizens with the importance of donating reproductive cells. The area of treatment for infertility will be governed by the rules and standards of the Council of Europe of the European Parliament and of the EU in this area by the procedures of biomedically assisted fertilisation. The law is also in line with the Convention on Human Rights in Biomedicine and the Framework Protocol implementing the Convention on Human Rights in the Biomedicine of the Council of Europe. On a proposal from the Ministry of Health, the Republic Health Insurance Institute has been funding an out-of-body fertilisation attempt by the Republic Health Insurance Authority since July 2006. The professional criteria were proposed by the Republic's expert extraterrestrial fertilisation and assisted reproduction Commission. The programme is being conducted at the Institute of Gynaecology and Accumulation of the Clinical Centre of Serbia, at the National Front of Belgrade and at the Clinical Centre of Vojvodina, and since September 2008 in six private health institutions. During 2007, a total of 699 successful out-of-body fertilisations were made in the three mentioned state institutions, and then in 2008 a total of nine institutions, three in state and six in private ownership, 1.156 successful out-of-body fertilisations. In 2007, the Republic Health Insurance Office invested around 197m dinars in these proceedings, and in 2008, 325,964,322 dinars. In this area, the percentage of success in Serbia in these two years is 32%, in Great Britain is 28%, in Slovenia 36%, in Hungary 31%. An egg cell donation was conducted only at the Institute of Gastronomy and Accumulation of the Clinical Centre of Serbia in a number of 1.24%, in 25 of the 2,024 attempts. During the public debate that lasted, I can freely say, for more than four months, with the availability of all solutions and on open round tables organised according to the plan approved by the government in Nis, and in Kragujevac, both in Novi Sad and Belgrade, and then during the discussion by electronic means, because all the draft laws were available at the health ministry site, there were different tones of some solutions. When we talk about out-of-body fertilization, as we call this area, it's actually a law on the treatment of infertility. This means that he deals with the regulation of the treatment of infertility by the procedures of biomedically assisted fertilisation, when women and men are married or in an out-of-marriage community. Serbia's family law prescribes the definition of family from the aspect of parental responsibility, so understanding the concept of family has no other way. If it were defined by this law, it would not be right in the context of the existing Family Law. In Croatia, for example, a few days ago, parliament passed a law that treats only the marriage community from the corner of treating infertility through biomedically assisted fertilisation. If the argument were to be respected in some respects, that even individual women or men living alone can be treated in this way as the law provides, in this case it could only be achieved in view of the gender equality of the introduction of surrogate motherhood, and against surrogate motherhood was the majority of the profession and the majority of the argument during the public debate. In other words, I would repeat that this law on the treatment of infertility is a biomedically assisted fertilization procedure, and all others, very complex and certainly a large part of the issue, would have to be dealt with by legislation on the family and then by a new regulation that would regulate, other than the treatment of infertility that is defined and regulated by this law, and something else. The laws we are proposing today represent a way to first regulate important areas that are very dynamic and demanding. The adoption of the law is the way in which in the area of blood transfusions, organ transplants, cell and tissue transplants, treatment of infertility through biomedically assisted fertilisation procedures, and get even better results, to the benefit of citizens and patients in need of assistance in these areas.
